        Idea:
vibecomfy: warm-runtime sessions (keep models resident across generations by reusing Comfy's existing model_management, not reinventing it).

Source repo: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy
Embedded ComfyUI fork in use: hiddenswitch (path: /Users/user_c042661f/.cache/uv/git-v0/checkouts/b790d0c34622492a/c5ed9402/)

# Problem
Today both vibecomfy runtime paths are cold-per-call:
- Embedded: vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:125-138 enters/exits a fresh Comfy() context per run. The exit calls embedded_comfy_client.py:_cleanup -> model_management.unload_all_models() + gc + soft_empty_cache. Models are dumped after every generation.
- Managed server: vibecomfy/runtime/server.py:16 starts comfyui as a subprocess, queues once, terminates. Same outcome at the process level.

ComfyUI itself already keeps models warm by default — current_loaded_models persists in process, load_models_gpu reuses resident copies, eviction is pressure-driven, OOM is auto-handled (execution.py:735-737), and DISABLE_SMART_MEMORY is the only thing that forces unload after each prompt. So the work is structural: stop tearing the runtime down.

# Goal
Add session-aware warm runtime to vibecomfy with two backends sharing one abstraction:
1. Long-lived managed Comfy server (HTTP control plane) — default for CLI users.
2. Long-lived embedded Comfy() context — for in-process callers (runpod worker, tests, tight matrix loops).

Default policy: keep warm. Explicit flush available. Auto-flush on detected model-set change under VRAM pressure.

# Verified facts (don't re-research)
- POST /free and POST /api/free both exist in the embedded hiddenswitch fork at comfy/cmd/server.py:1041 with /api mirror auto-added at :1357. Payload: {"unload_models": bool, "free_memory": bool}. They set queue flags via prompt_queue.set_flag(...) — async, take effect at next prompt boundary, not synchronously.
- CLI flags exist in comfy/cli_args.py:115-160: --highvram, --lowvram, --normalvram, --reserve-vram <GB>, --disable-smart-memory, --cache-classic, --cache-lru N, --cache-none.
- Comfy.clear_cache() (embedded_comfy_client.py:385) and Comfy.reconfigure() (:398) already exposed; reconfigure uses MODEL_MANAGEMENT_ARGS fingerprint to avoid unnecessary subprocess cycling.
- The cleanup that nukes warm state lives in embedded_comfy_client.py:_cleanup (lines ~149-169) and is called from Comfy.__aexit__ (line ~481).

# Design constraints / refinements over the user's notes
- Do NOT default to --highvram (breaks multi-model workflows that need offload). Default to Comfy's smart memory.
- /free is asynchronous — flush API must either drain via a no-op prompt or document the queue-flag semantics. Tests must account for this.
- Auto-flush should only trigger on model-set fingerprint change AND VRAM-headroom-below-threshold. Input/seed/prompt changes must NOT trigger flush.
- VIBECOMFY_WARM=auto|always|never controls policy (auto default).
- Session lifecycle: daemon-style for CLI (pidfile + URL file under out/sessions/<id>/) so multiple `vibecomfy run` invocations share one warm pool. Embedded session is just a Python context manager.
- Browser-only tasks: there are none in this plan (no UI work).
- No hard-halt prereq clauses: each milestone is independently shippable.

# Out of scope for this megaplan
- Multi-GPU session pooling.
- Distributed/remote session sharing across machines.
- Workflow-graph-level caching (Comfy already has --cache-lru / --cache-classic).
- Replacing the existing one-shot run/run_embedded APIs — they should remain as thin wrappers around the new session.
- Cloud/RunPod orchestration changes (the runpod worker can adopt EmbeddedSession in a separate plan).

# Suggested milestone shape (megaplan can refine during prep/plan)
M1: Session abstraction + embedded backend
  - vibecomfy/runtime/session.py: VibeSession protocol + EmbeddedSession holding a long-lived Comfy() across multiple .run(workflow) calls.
  - .flush() -> Comfy.clear_cache(); .reconfigure(config) -> Comfy.reconfigure().
  - Refactor run_embedded() to be a one-shot wrapper around EmbeddedSession.
  - Tests: two sequential runs in one session reuse models (assert via inspecting comfy.model_management.current_loaded_models after each run, or via a stub model manager); flush() drops them; reconfigure with same fingerprint is a no-op.

M2: Managed server backend
  - ServerSession: keeps comfy_server subprocess alive across .run() calls. Start = spawn + wait-ready. .flush() = POST /api/free with appropriate flags. .stop() = SIGTERM with timeout fallback to SIGKILL.
  - Daemon mode: `vibecomfy session start [--id NAME] [--vram-policy ...]` writes pidfile + URL to out/sessions/<id>/; `vibecomfy session stop <id>`; `vibecomfy session list`; `vibecomfy session flush <id>`.
  - `vibecomfy run` auto-discovers active session if one exists, else falls back to one-shot managed mode.
  - Tests: session start/stop, flush via HTTP, two runs share warmth, daemon survives across CLI invocations.

M3: Auto-flush policy + fingerprinting
  - vibecomfy/runtime/model_fingerprint.py: extract loader-node identity tuples from a workflow (CheckpointLoaderSimple.ckpt_name, LoraLoader.lora_name, UNETLoader.unet_name, VAELoader.vae_name, CLIPLoader.clip_name, DualCLIPLoader, etc. — discover the canonical set from the embedded fork's nodes).
  - Session.run() compares current workflow fingerprint to last-run fingerprint. If different AND free VRAM < threshold (configurable, default e.g. 2GB), call flush() before queueing.
  - VIBECOMFY_WARM env var: auto (default) | always | never.
  - Tests: same model + different seed -> no flush; different ckpt -> flush; auto-flush gated by VRAM headroom (mock get_free_memory).

M4: VRAM/cache flag plumbing + docs
  - Session config accepts: vram_policy ("auto"|"high"|"low"|"normal"), reserve_vram_gb, cache_policy ("smart"|"classic"|"lru:N"|"none"), disable_smart_memory.
  - Threaded through to embedded Configuration AND server CLI args.
  - docs/runtime_lifecycle.md: add "Warm sessions" section. docs/runtime_surface.md: document VibeSession API.
  - Smoke test: `vibecomfy session start && vibecomfy run x.json && vibecomfy run y.json && vibecomfy session stop` — second run is materially faster than first (timing-based, with tolerance).

# Open questions for the planning step
- Should `vibecomfy run` lazily start a session on first call and reuse it (implicit warm) vs always require `session start`? Lean toward explicit start for predictability.
- Does the runpod worker (reigh-workspace/reigh-worker) want to consume EmbeddedSession directly or talk to a local managed session? Probably embedded — it owns the process lifecycle already. Out of scope for this plan, but the API should not preclude it.
- Do we need partial flush (just CLIP, just VAE, just UNet)? Probably no — Comfy's pressure-driven eviction handles per-model decisions; only expose all-or-nothing flush at the session boundary.

# Success criteria (suggested — planning phase should refine)
- A `VibeSession` abstraction exists with both Embedded and Server backends.
- Two sequential `.run()` calls in one session share warm models (verified by test, not just timing).
- `flush()` is explicit and works for both backends. For the server backend, the test accounts for /free's queue-flag semantics.
- `vibecomfy session start/stop/list/flush` CLI commands exist and survive across separate `vibecomfy` invocations.
- Auto-flush policy fires on model-set change under VRAM pressure, never on seed/input changes.
- Existing `run` and `run_embedded` one-shot APIs continue to work unchanged (backwards compatibility).
- Documentation updated in docs/runtime_lifecycle.md and docs/runtime_surface.md.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Warm-runtime VibeSession (embedded + managed backends)

## Overview

Today both runtime entry points are cold-per-call: `run_embedded()` enters/exits a fresh `Comfy()` per generation (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:125-138`) whose `__aexit__` triggers `unload_all_models()`, and `run()` spawns/terminates `comfyui serve` as a subprocess per call (`vibecomfy/runtime/server.py:27-50`). ComfyUI itself already keeps models warm in-process — the work here is structural.

We add a `VibeSession` abstraction with two backends (`EmbeddedSession` keeping a long-lived `Comfy()`, `ServerSession` keeping a long-lived `comfyui serve` subprocess), plus a session-pool daemon flow on the CLI side (`out/sessions/<id>/`) so multiple `vibecomfy run` invocations share one warm pool. Existing `run`/`run_embedded` remain as one-shot wrappers. Default policy is keep-warm with smart memory; explicit `flush()` and a model-fingerprint-gated auto-flush handle model swaps under VRAM pressure.

Constraints: `/free` is async (queue flag) — tests must reflect; don't default to `--highvram`; auto-flush gated on **fingerprint change AND** free-VRAM-below-threshold; embedded fork not installed locally — keep the existing `sys.modules` stub pattern from `tests/test_runtime_run.py:140-149`.

## Phase 1: Embedded session foundation

### Step 1: Add VibeSession protocol + EmbeddedSession (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` with `SessionConfig` dataclass (vram_policy, reserve_vram_gb, cache_policy, disable_smart_memory, warm_policy, auto_flush_vram_threshold_gb=2.0), `VibeSession` Protocol (start/run/flush/reconfigure/stop/last_fingerprint), and `EmbeddedSession` holding one long-lived `Comfy()` across `.run()` calls. `flush()` → `comfy.clear_cache()`; `reconfigure()` → `comfy.reconfigure(...)` (uses MODEL_MANAGEMENT_ARGS fingerprint so identical configs are no-ops); `stop()` swallows the `model_mmap_residency` AttributeError currently handled at `run.py:135-138`.
2. **Move** `_embedded_configuration` from `runtime/run.py:163-181` into `session.py` and extend it to apply `SessionConfig` fields (vram policy → highvram/lowvram/normalvram, reserve_vram_gb, cache_classic/cache_lru/cache_none, disable_smart_memory). Keep `VIBECOMFY_COMFY_CONFIGURATION` env override last.
3. **Add** `model_fingerprint(api_dict) -> tuple` walking the api_dict and collecting `(class_type, model_field_value)` pairs for: `CheckpointLoaderSimple.ckpt_name`, `CheckpointLoader.ckpt_name`, `LoraLoader.lora_name`, `LoraLoaderModelOnly.lora_name`, `UNETLoader.unet_name`, `VAELoader.vae_name`, `CLIPLoader.clip_name`, `DualCLIPLoader.clip_name1`+`clip_name2`, `CLIPVisionLoader.clip_name`, `ControlNetLoader.control_net_name`, `UpscaleModelLoader.model_name`. Sorted tuple. Fingerprints over values, not node ids.

### Step 2: Auto-flush gate inside EmbeddedSession.run (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **In** `EmbeddedSession.run()` before queueing: if `warm_policy == "never"` flush; if `"always"` never auto-flush; if `"auto"` and `self.last_fingerprint is not None and fp != self.last_fingerprint and _free_vram_gb() < threshold` → flush. After successful queue: `self.last_fingerprint = fp`.
2. **Add** `_free_vram_gb()` lazy-importing `comfy.model_management.get_free_memory()`, returning `float("inf")` on failure (mac mps dev box won't crash).
3. **Honor** `VIBECOMFY_WARM` env override (auto|always|never).

### Step 3: Refactor run_embedded into a one-shot wrapper (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Rewrite** `run_embedded()` (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:116-156`) as a thin wrapper that calls `EmbeddedSession.start()/run()/stop()`. Public signature unchanged. `run_embedded_sync` unchanged.

### Step 4: Tests for the embedded session (`tests/test_runtime_session.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `tests/test_runtime_session.py` using the `sys.modules` injection pattern from `tests/test_runtime_run.py:140-149`. `FakeComfy` records `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`/`queue_prompt_api`/`clear_cache`/`reconfigure`. Cover: two runs share one enter/exit pair, `flush()` calls `clear_cache` once, `reconfigure(SessionConfig)` translates fields correctly, fingerprint-change-under-pressure triggers flush but seed-only changes don't, `VIBECOMFY_WARM=never|always` honored, existing `test_runtime_run.py` still passes.

## Phase 2: Managed server session

### Step 5: ServerSession backend (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`, `vibecomfy/runtime/client.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `ServerSession` to `session.py`: `start()` spawns `comfyui serve` once (extract `_spawn_comfy_server(config)` from `runtime/server.py:27-41`) with `SessionConfig` translated to CLI flags (`--highvram|--lowvram|--normalvram`, `--reserve-vram`, `--disable-smart-memory`, `--cache-classic|--cache-lru N|--cache-none`), waits for `/system_stats` readiness. `run()` POSTs `/prompt` and applies the same auto-flush gate as Step 2. `flush()` POSTs `/api/free` with `{"unload_models": True, "free_memory": True}`. `reconfigure(config)` is restart-on-change. `stop()` reuses the SIGTERM/15s/SIGKILL pattern from `server.py:44-50`.
2. **Add** `ComfyClient.free(unload_models, free_memory)` in `runtime/client.py:30+` (1-line docstring noting queue-flag async semantics).

### Step 6: Daemon CLI (`vibecomfy/commands/session.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `vibecomfy/commands/session.py` with `start/stop/list/flush/status` subcommands. Daemonize by `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-m", "vibecomfy.commands._session_daemon", ...], start_new_session=True)`. Daemon writes `out/sessions/<id>/{pid,url,config.json}`, sleeps until SIGTERM, removes pidfile.
2. **Add** `runtime/session.py::find_active_session(id="default") -> str | None` reading `out/sessions/<id>/url` if pid alive.
3. **Register** in `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py:14-28`. Update `vibecomfy/commands/run.py:25-31`: for `--runtime server` with no `--server-url`, auto-attach to default session if alive; else current one-shot fallback.

### Step 7: Tests for ServerSession + CLI (`tests/test_runtime_session.py`, `tests/test_session_cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `ServerSession` tests with monkeypatched subprocess + stubbed httpx (the `test_runtime_run.py:71-95` pattern). Cover: CLI args reflect SessionConfig, two runs share one spawn, `flush()` POSTs `/api/free`, reconfigure restarts on change, stop() SIGTERMs then SIGKILLs.
2. **Create** `tests/test_session_cli.py` testing argparse wiring (start/stop/list/flush/status) with daemon spawn mocked to a no-op that writes pidfile.

## Phase 3: Documentation + smoke

### Step 8: Docs (`docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`, `docs/runtime_surface.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Append** "Warm sessions" section to `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md` (Embedded vs Server, `vibecomfy session` CLI, warm-policy / auto-flush rules, `/api/free` queue-flag semantics, `VIBECOMFY_WARM`).
2. **Append** `VibeSession` API block to `docs/runtime_surface.md` under "Embedded Surface".

### Step 9: Smoke (`scripts/warm_session_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a manual smoke script running two cheap workflows back-to-back via one session, printing elapsed deltas. Not in CI (dev box has no NVIDIA per `docs/runtime_profile.md:9-10`).

## Execution Order
1. Steps 1–4: embedded session + tests first (touches existing public surface).
2. Steps 5–7: ServerSession + daemon CLI.
3. Step 8: docs.
4. Step 9: smoke script.
Each phase is independently shippable (no hard-halt prereqs).

## Validation Order
1. `pytest tests/test_runtime_run.py` — refactor preserves behavior.
2. `pytest tests/test_runtime_session.py` — new session tests with stubbed comfy.
3. `pytest tests/test_session_cli.py` — daemon CLI flow.
4. Full `pytest` — no collateral damage.
5. Manual on RunPod (info-only): `vibecomfy session start && time vibecomfy run x.json && time vibecomfy run x.json && vibecomfy session stop default`.

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "concern": "is the change in the right place, and would it break any callers?: the optional cleanup steps are not in the right place yet. `pairregionslayer` does not receive `pairdatabyindex` today; its props are only `images`, `imagepositionswithpending`, `pairinfowithpending`, and callback/display props in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/timeline/timelinecontainer/components/pairregionslayer.tsx](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/timeline/timelinecontainer/components/pairregionslayer.tsx#l20). so step 4's 'pass `pairdatabyindex.get(pairindex)` directly' would require new prop plumbing or a different seam.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

  ],
  "is-the-scope-and-scale-of-the-change-appropriate": [

      "concern": "is the scope and scale of the change appropriate?: phase 2 is still under-specified for execution. step 4 says pairregionslayer should either pass `pairdatabyindex.get(pairindex)` directly or 'just pass the index + frame-only data', which are materially different designs. if phase 2 is kept in the plan, it needs a single concrete direction.",

      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: the plan still lacks an explicit automated regression test for the reported bug. step 2 is manual ('check that `usevideoregeneratemode` now gets the fresh url'), but there is no concrete test that opens a segment slot, changes the primary variant data, and asserts that the regenerate path sees the fresh `url`, `generationid`, and `primaryvariantid`.",

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.

> DEVELOPER

You are already running inside the megaplan harness for this step. Do the requested planning/review/execution work directly. Do NOT invoke the `megaplan` CLI, do NOT read or activate the `megaplan` skill, do NOT start nested megaplan plans, and do NOT recurse into another planning harness. Treat mentions of megaplan in the repository or environment as implementation context only.

You are an independent reviewer. Critique the plan against the actual repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy





        Idea:
vibecomfy: warm-runtime sessions (keep models resident across generations by reusing Comfy's existing model_management, not reinventing it).

Source repo: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy
Embedded ComfyUI fork in use: hiddenswitch (path: /Users/user_c042661f/.cache/uv/git-v0/checkouts/b790d0c34622492a/c5ed9402/)

# Problem
Today both vibecomfy runtime paths are cold-per-call:
- Embedded: vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:125-138 enters/exits a fresh Comfy() context per run. The exit calls embedded_comfy_client.py:_cleanup -> model_management.unload_all_models() + gc + soft_empty_cache. Models are dumped after every generation.
- Managed server: vibecomfy/runtime/server.py:16 starts comfyui as a subprocess, queues once, terminates. Same outcome at the process level.

ComfyUI itself already keeps models warm by default — current_loaded_models persists in process, load_models_gpu reuses resident copies, eviction is pressure-driven, OOM is auto-handled (execution.py:735-737), and DISABLE_SMART_MEMORY is the only thing that forces unload after each prompt. So the work is structural: stop tearing the runtime down.

# Goal
Add session-aware warm runtime to vibecomfy with two backends sharing one abstraction:
1. Long-lived managed Comfy server (HTTP control plane) — default for CLI users.
2. Long-lived embedded Comfy() context — for in-process callers (runpod worker, tests, tight matrix loops).

Default policy: keep warm. Explicit flush available. Auto-flush on detected model-set change under VRAM pressure.

# Verified facts (don't re-research)
- POST /free and POST /api/free both exist in the embedded hiddenswitch fork at comfy/cmd/server.py:1041 with /api mirror auto-added at :1357. Payload: {"unload_models": bool, "free_memory": bool}. They set queue flags via prompt_queue.set_flag(...) — async, take effect at next prompt boundary, not synchronously.
- CLI flags exist in comfy/cli_args.py:115-160: --highvram, --lowvram, --normalvram, --reserve-vram <GB>, --disable-smart-memory, --cache-classic, --cache-lru N, --cache-none.
- Comfy.clear_cache() (embedded_comfy_client.py:385) and Comfy.reconfigure() (:398) already exposed; reconfigure uses MODEL_MANAGEMENT_ARGS fingerprint to avoid unnecessary subprocess cycling.
- The cleanup that nukes warm state lives in embedded_comfy_client.py:_cleanup (lines ~149-169) and is called from Comfy.__aexit__ (line ~481).

# Design constraints / refinements over the user's notes
- Do NOT default to --highvram (breaks multi-model workflows that need offload). Default to Comfy's smart memory.
- /free is asynchronous — flush API must either drain via a no-op prompt or document the queue-flag semantics. Tests must account for this.
- Auto-flush should only trigger on model-set fingerprint change AND VRAM-headroom-below-threshold. Input/seed/prompt changes must NOT trigger flush.
- VIBECOMFY_WARM=auto|always|never controls policy (auto default).
- Session lifecycle: daemon-style for CLI (pidfile + URL file under out/sessions/<id>/) so multiple `vibecomfy run` invocations share one warm pool. Embedded session is just a Python context manager.
- Browser-only tasks: there are none in this plan (no UI work).
- No hard-halt prereq clauses: each milestone is independently shippable.

# Out of scope for this megaplan
- Multi-GPU session pooling.
- Distributed/remote session sharing across machines.
- Workflow-graph-level caching (Comfy already has --cache-lru / --cache-classic).
- Replacing the existing one-shot run/run_embedded APIs — they should remain as thin wrappers around the new session.
- Cloud/RunPod orchestration changes (the runpod worker can adopt EmbeddedSession in a separate plan).

# Suggested milestone shape (megaplan can refine during prep/plan)
M1: Session abstraction + embedded backend
  - vibecomfy/runtime/session.py: VibeSession protocol + EmbeddedSession holding a long-lived Comfy() across multiple .run(workflow) calls.
  - .flush() -> Comfy.clear_cache(); .reconfigure(config) -> Comfy.reconfigure().
  - Refactor run_embedded() to be a one-shot wrapper around EmbeddedSession.
  - Tests: two sequential runs in one session reuse models (assert via inspecting comfy.model_management.current_loaded_models after each run, or via a stub model manager); flush() drops them; reconfigure with same fingerprint is a no-op.

M2: Managed server backend
  - ServerSession: keeps comfy_server subprocess alive across .run() calls. Start = spawn + wait-ready. .flush() = POST /api/free with appropriate flags. .stop() = SIGTERM with timeout fallback to SIGKILL.
  - Daemon mode: `vibecomfy session start [--id NAME] [--vram-policy ...]` writes pidfile + URL to out/sessions/<id>/; `vibecomfy session stop <id>`; `vibecomfy session list`; `vibecomfy session flush <id>`.
  - `vibecomfy run` auto-discovers active session if one exists, else falls back to one-shot managed mode.
  - Tests: session start/stop, flush via HTTP, two runs share warmth, daemon survives across CLI invocations.

M3: Auto-flush policy + fingerprinting
  - vibecomfy/runtime/model_fingerprint.py: extract loader-node identity tuples from a workflow (CheckpointLoaderSimple.ckpt_name, LoraLoader.lora_name, UNETLoader.unet_name, VAELoader.vae_name, CLIPLoader.clip_name, DualCLIPLoader, etc. — discover the canonical set from the embedded fork's nodes).
  - Session.run() compares current workflow fingerprint to last-run fingerprint. If different AND free VRAM < threshold (configurable, default e.g. 2GB), call flush() before queueing.
  - VIBECOMFY_WARM env var: auto (default) | always | never.
  - Tests: same model + different seed -> no flush; different ckpt -> flush; auto-flush gated by VRAM headroom (mock get_free_memory).

M4: VRAM/cache flag plumbing + docs
  - Session config accepts: vram_policy ("auto"|"high"|"low"|"normal"), reserve_vram_gb, cache_policy ("smart"|"classic"|"lru:N"|"none"), disable_smart_memory.
  - Threaded through to embedded Configuration AND server CLI args.
  - docs/runtime_lifecycle.md: add "Warm sessions" section. docs/runtime_surface.md: document VibeSession API.
  - Smoke test: `vibecomfy session start && vibecomfy run x.json && vibecomfy run y.json && vibecomfy session stop` — second run is materially faster than first (timing-based, with tolerance).

# Open questions for the planning step
- Should `vibecomfy run` lazily start a session on first call and reuse it (implicit warm) vs always require `session start`? Lean toward explicit start for predictability.
- Does the runpod worker (reigh-workspace/reigh-worker) want to consume EmbeddedSession directly or talk to a local managed session? Probably embedded — it owns the process lifecycle already. Out of scope for this plan, but the API should not preclude it.
- Do we need partial flush (just CLIP, just VAE, just UNet)? Probably no — Comfy's pressure-driven eviction handles per-model decisions; only expose all-or-nothing flush at the session boundary.

# Success criteria (suggested — planning phase should refine)
- A `VibeSession` abstraction exists with both Embedded and Server backends.
- Two sequential `.run()` calls in one session share warm models (verified by test, not just timing).
- `flush()` is explicit and works for both backends. For the server backend, the test accounts for /free's queue-flag semantics.
- `vibecomfy session start/stop/list/flush` CLI commands exist and survive across separate `vibecomfy` invocations.
- Auto-flush policy fires on model-set change under VRAM pressure, never on seed/input changes.
- Existing `run` and `run_embedded` one-shot APIs continue to work unchanged (backwards compatibility).
- Documentation updated in docs/runtime_lifecycle.md and docs/runtime_surface.md.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Warm-runtime VibeSession (embedded + managed backends)

## Overview

Both runtime entry points are cold-per-call today: `run_embedded()` enters/exits a fresh `Comfy()` per generation (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:125-138`) whose `__aexit__` runs `unload_all_models()`, and `run()` spawns/terminates `comfyui serve` per call (`vibecomfy/runtime/server.py:27-50`). ComfyUI itself keeps models warm in-process and evicts under pressure — the work here is structural.

We add a `VibeSession` abstraction with two backends — `EmbeddedSession` (one long-lived `Comfy()`) and `ServerSession` (one long-lived `comfyui serve` subprocess) — plus a CLI session pool under `out/sessions/<id>/` (pidfile + URL file) so multiple `vibecomfy run` invocations share one warm pool. Existing `run`/`run_embedded` stay as one-shot wrappers (backwards-compatible).

Per the brief, the long-lived **managed** server is the default for CLI users. The plan changes `vibecomfy run`'s default runtime from `embedded` to `auto` and routes through a discovered session when one exists. The schema provider is taught to share the same URL so we don't accidentally spin up a second cold subprocess for `/object_info`. Default policy: keep-warm with smart memory; explicit `flush()` and a fingerprint-gated auto-flush handle model swaps under VRAM pressure.

Key constraints kept from the brief:
- `/free` is async (queue flag) — `ServerSession.flush()` documents this; tests assert payload + endpoint, not synchronous unload.
- Don't default to `--highvram`.
- Auto-flush gated on **fingerprint change AND** free-VRAM-below-threshold; seed/prompt/input changes never trigger flush.
- Embedded fork not installed locally — tests use the existing `sys.modules` stub pattern from `tests/test_runtime_run.py:140-149`.

Critique resolutions baked into this revision:
- **FLAG-001 / correctness-1**: Compiled API workflows in this repo store loader filenames under positional widget keys (`widget_0`, `widget_1`, ...) — confirmed in `tests/snapshots/wan_t2v.api.json:30-49` and `vibecomfy/blocks/loaders.py:27-47` (compile merges `node.widgets` at `vibecomfy/workflow.py:178`). The fingerprint extractor must read both canonical names AND positional `widget_*` slots for known loader classes.
- **FLAG-002 / issue_hints**: `vibecomfy run` default changes from `embedded` to `auto`; `auto` prefers a discovered warm session, then falls back to one-shot embedded for backwards compatibility.
- **FLAG-003 / correctness-2**: Managed-runtime warmth is verified at the **session-level** (test asserts spawn count == 1 for N runs and `/api/free` calls) and via embedded `comfy.queue_prompt_api()` which already waits for completion. The optional manual GPU smoke uses the embedded session (which waits for outputs), not `time vibecomfy run --runtime server`. We do not add `/history` polling to v1.
- **FLAG-004 / scope / callers**: `_cmd_run` discovers the session URL **before** constructing the schema provider and threads it via `get_schema_provider("auto", server_url=session_url)` (already supported, see `vibecomfy/schema/provider.py:161-178`). No second cold runtime is spawned for object_info when a warm session is reachable.
- **all_locations**: Daemon is folded into `vibecomfy/commands/session.py` (single file, with a `_run_daemon` entry invoked via `python -m vibecomfy.commands.session --daemon`). No separate `_session_daemon.py` is created.
- Open planning questions resolved inline: auto-flush threshold default = 2.0 GB (configurable); ServerSession default port = 8188 with `--port` override.

Out of scope: multi-GPU pooling, distributed sessions, workflow-graph caching, replacing one-shot APIs, RunPod orchestration changes.

## Phase 1: Embedded session foundation

### Step 1: Add VibeSession protocol + EmbeddedSession (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` with:
   - `SessionConfig` dataclass: `vram_policy: Literal["auto","high","low","normal"] = "auto"`, `reserve_vram_gb: float | None = None`, `cache_policy: Literal["smart","classic","none"] | str = "smart"` (also accepts `"lru:N"`), `disable_smart_memory: bool = False`, `warm_policy: Literal["auto","always","never"] = "auto"`, `auto_flush_vram_threshold_gb: float = 2.0`, `port: int = 8188` (server-only).
   - `class VibeSession(Protocol)`: `async start()`, `async run(workflow, *, backend="api") -> RunResult`, `async flush(*, unload_models=True, free_memory=True)`, `async reconfigure(config)`, `async stop()`, attribute `last_fingerprint`.
   - `class EmbeddedSession(VibeSession)`: holds one long-lived `Comfy()` across calls. `start()` instantiates and `__aenter__`s once. `run()` calls `comfy.queue_prompt_api(api_dict)` (which already waits for completion per `docs/runtime_surface.md:42-46`). `flush()` → `comfy.clear_cache()`. `reconfigure(SessionConfig)` → `comfy.reconfigure(...)` (uses `MODEL_MANAGEMENT_ARGS` fingerprint, so identical configs are no-ops). `stop()` calls `__aexit__(None, None, None)` and tolerates the `model_mmap_residency` AttributeError currently swallowed at `run.py:135-138`.
2. **Move** `_embedded_configuration` from `runtime/run.py:163-181` into `session.py` and extend it to apply `SessionConfig` (vram_policy → highvram/lowvram/normalvram booleans, `reserve_vram_gb` → `reserve_vram`, `cache_policy` → `cache_classic`/`cache_lru`/`cache_none`, `disable_smart_memory`). `VIBECOMFY_COMFY_CONFIGURATION` env override applies last.
3. **Add** model fingerprinting (`session.py::model_fingerprint(api_dict) -> tuple`). Walks `api_dict` and emits sorted `(class_type, slot, value)` triples for the canonical loader set. **Both canonical input names AND positional `widget_*` slots** are read because `VibeWorkflow.compile()` merges `node.widgets` into inputs as `widget_0/1/...` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:176-179`). Loader → slots map (v1, hard-coded):
   - `CheckpointLoaderSimple` / `CheckpointLoader`: `ckpt_name`, `widget_0`
   - `LoraLoader` / `LoraLoaderModelOnly`: `lora_name`, `widget_0`
   - `UNETLoader`: `unet_name`, `widget_0`
   - `VAELoader`: `vae_name`, `widget_0`
   - `CLIPLoader`: `clip_name`, `widget_0`
   - `DualCLIPLoader`: `clip_name1`, `clip_name2`, `widget_0`, `widget_1`
   - `CLIPVisionLoader`: `clip_name`, `widget_0`
   - `ControlNetLoader`: `control_net_name`, `widget_0`
   - `UpscaleModelLoader`: `model_name`, `widget_0`
   - For each match, only include slots whose value is a non-empty `str` (filters out integers like KSampler `widget_0` seeds).
   The fingerprint is a tuple of `(class_type, slot, value)` triples sorted lexicographically — node-id reshuffles don't trigger flush.

### Step 2: Auto-flush gate inside `EmbeddedSession.run` (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Compute** `fp = model_fingerprint(api_dict)` before queueing. Resolve effective policy: `os.environ.get("VIBECOMFY_WARM")` overrides `config.warm_policy` if set.
   - `never` → `await self.flush()` then queue.
   - `always` → never auto-flush.
   - `auto` → if `self.last_fingerprint is not None and fp != self.last_fingerprint and _free_vram_gb() < config.auto_flush_vram_threshold_gb`, `await self.flush()` first.
2. After successful queue: `self.last_fingerprint = fp`.
3. **Add** `_free_vram_gb()` lazy-importing `comfy.model_management.get_free_memory()`, converting bytes → GB. Returns `float("inf")` on import/AttributeError (mac mps dev box won't crash; the `auto` gate becomes effectively no-op without GPU bookkeeping).

### Step 3: Refactor `run_embedded` into a one-shot wrapper (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Rewrite** `run_embedded()` (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:116-156`) as:
   ```python
   session = EmbeddedSession(SessionConfig.from_workflow_metadata(workflow))
   await session.start()
   try:
       return await session.run(workflow, backend=backend)
   finally:
       await session.stop()
   ```
   `run_embedded_sync` and the public signature stay unchanged. The `model_mmap_residency` swallowing moves into `EmbeddedSession.stop()` so existing `test_run_embedded_ignores_hiddenswitch_cleanup_bug_after_success` passes.

### Step 4: Tests for the embedded session (`tests/test_runtime_session.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tests/test_runtime_session.py` reusing the `sys.modules` injection pattern from `tests/test_runtime_run.py:140-149`. `FakeComfy` records enter/exit counts plus `queue_prompt_api`/`clear_cache`/`reconfigure` calls.
2. **Cover (must)**:
   - Two `session.run(workflow_a)` calls produce **one** `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` pair.
   - `flush()` calls `clear_cache()` exactly once.
   - `reconfigure(SessionConfig(...))` passes a Configuration whose fields reflect SessionConfig (vram_policy → highvram/lowvram/normalvram, reserve_vram, cache_classic/lru/none, disable_smart_memory).
   - **Fingerprint covers `widget_*` slots**: load `tests/snapshots/wan_t2v.api.json` (UNETLoader/CLIPLoader/VAELoader values under `widget_0`) — same dict twice → same fingerprint; mutate `widget_0` of `UNETLoader` → different fingerprint. Same test asserts canonical names like `unet_name` are also picked up when present.
   - Auto-flush truth table with mocked `_free_vram_gb`:
     - same fingerprint, low VRAM → no flush.
     - different fingerprint, low VRAM (0.5 GB) → flush.
     - different fingerprint, high VRAM (10 GB) → no flush.
     - same `ckpt_name`, different KSampler seed (`widget_0` integer in non-loader node) → no flush.
   - `VIBECOMFY_WARM=never` → flush before every run regardless of headroom.
   - `VIBECOMFY_WARM=always` → never auto-flushes regardless of headroom or fingerprint change.
3. **Confirm** existing `tests/test_runtime_run.py` still passes after the `run_embedded` refactor.

## Phase 2: Managed server session + warm-default CLI

### Step 5: ServerSession backend (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`, `vibecomfy/runtime/client.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Extract** the subprocess startup from `runtime/server.py:27-41` into a private helper `_spawn_comfy_server(config) -> tuple[Process, str, log_handle]` that translates `SessionConfig` to CLI flags: `--highvram|--lowvram|--normalvram` (skip when `auto`), `--reserve-vram <GB>`, `--disable-smart-memory`, `--cache-classic|--cache-lru N|--cache-none` (skip when `smart`), `--port <P>`. Reuse the existing 120-iteration `/system_stats` readiness loop.
2. **Add** `ServerSession(VibeSession)` to `session.py`. `start()` calls `_spawn_comfy_server`, records `self.url`, `self.process`, `self.log_path`, `self._cli_args`. `run()` calls `ComfyClient(self.url).queue_prompt(api_dict)` and applies the same auto-flush gate as Step 2 (calls `flush()` before queue if fingerprint changed under pressure). `flush()` POSTs `/api/free` with `{"unload_models": True, "free_memory": True}`. `reconfigure(config)` is restart-on-change: rebuild `_cli_args`; if identical, no-op; else `await self.stop(); await self.start()`. `stop()` reuses the SIGTERM/15s/SIGKILL pattern from `server.py:44-50`.
3. **Add** `ComfyClient.free(unload_models: bool, free_memory: bool)` in `runtime/client.py:30+`. One-line docstring: "POST `/api/free`. Server flag is queue-async; takes effect at next prompt boundary."
4. **Note** in the docstring of `ServerSession.flush`: warmth verification at the test layer is by spawn count + endpoint payload, not by synchronous unload.

### Step 6: Daemon CLI + warm-default routing (`vibecomfy/commands/session.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/run.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `vibecomfy/commands/session.py` containing the argparse wiring for `start/stop/list/flush/status` AND the in-process daemon entrypoint. Single file — no separate `_session_daemon.py`. The module supports being run as `python -m vibecomfy.commands.session --daemon --id NAME --config <json>` (a hidden flag handled before normal argparse routing); when invoked that way it instantiates `ServerSession(SessionConfig.from_dict(...))`, calls `start()`, writes `out/sessions/<id>/{pid,url,config.json}`, installs a SIGTERM handler that calls `await session.stop()` and removes the pidfile, then sleeps on a `asyncio.Event`.
2. **Daemonize** from `session start` by `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-m", "vibecomfy.commands.session", "--daemon", "--id", id_, "--config", json.dumps(config)], stdout=DEVNULL, stderr=open(log_path,"ab"), start_new_session=True)`. Parent waits up to 120 s for `out/sessions/<id>/url` to appear, then exits 0; on timeout, kills the daemon and exits 1.
3. **Subcommands**: `start [--id NAME=default] [--vram-policy auto|high|low|normal] [--reserve-vram-gb F] [--cache-policy smart|classic|lru:N|none] [--disable-smart-memory] [--warm-policy auto|always|never] [--port P=8188]`, `stop <id>` (SIGTERM the recorded pid, wait for pidfile removal, fallback SIGKILL after 15 s), `list` (lists `out/sessions/*/url` whose pid is alive), `flush <id> [--unload-models/--no-unload-models] [--free-memory/--no-free-memory]` (POST `/api/free` via `ComfyClient.free`), `status <id>` (prints url, pid, config).
4. **Add** `runtime/session.py::find_active_session(id="default") -> str | None`: read `out/sessions/<id>/url`; if pid file present and `os.kill(pid, 0)` succeeds, return the URL; else return `None`. Stale pidfile cleanup: if `os.kill` raises `ProcessLookupError`, remove the directory.
5. **Register** in `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py:14-28` by adding `CommandSpec("session", "vibecomfy.commands.session")`.
6. **Make warm-managed the default for `vibecomfy run`** (`vibecomfy/commands/run.py`):
   - Change `--runtime` default from `"embedded"` to `"auto"` (existing `"embedded"` and `"server"` values still accepted explicitly).
   - At the top of `_cmd_run`, **before** constructing the schema provider: discover the session URL via `find_active_session("default")` (only when `args.runtime in ("auto","server")` and `--server-url` not set). Resolve the effective runtime: `auto` + warm session → `server` against that URL; `auto` + no warm session → fall back to `embedded` one-shot (preserves backwards compatibility for users who never run `session start`); `server` + warm session → reuse it; `server` + no warm session → existing one-shot managed.
   - Pass the discovered/explicit URL into `get_schema_provider("auto", server_url=session_url)` so `RuntimeSchemaProvider.object_info_async()` (`vibecomfy/schema/provider.py:151-157`) reuses the warm session instead of spawning a fresh `comfy_server(...)`.
   - Pass the same URL into `run_sync(workflow, server_url=session_url, backend=...)` for the queue submission.

### Step 7: Tests for ServerSession + CLI (`tests/test_runtime_session.py`, `tests/test_session_cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **In** `tests/test_runtime_session.py` add `ServerSession` tests with monkeypatched `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` and a stub `httpx.AsyncClient` (the `tests/test_runtime_run.py:71-95` pattern). Cover (must):
   - `start()` invokes the subprocess with the expected CLI argv given `SessionConfig(vram_policy="high", reserve_vram_gb=2.0, cache_policy="lru:3", disable_smart_memory=True, port=8200)`.
   - Two `session.run(...)` calls share **one** subprocess (assert spawn count == 1 across N runs — addresses warm-managed verification at the test layer per FLAG-003).
   - `flush()` POSTs `/api/free` with `{"unload_models": True, "free_memory": True}`.
   - `reconfigure(same_config)` is a no-op (no second spawn). `reconfigure(different_cli_args)` triggers stop+start (spawn count increments).
   - `stop()` SIGTERMs once and falls back to SIGKILL when `wait()` exceeds the 15 s timeout.
2. **Create** `tests/test_session_cli.py`. Mock `subprocess.Popen` so the "daemon" is replaced by a synchronous helper that just writes `out/sessions/<id>/{pid,url,config.json}` with a synthetic alive pid (`os.getpid()` of the test process). Assert (must):
   - `vibecomfy session start` returns 0 and creates pidfile + url file under `out/sessions/default/` in `tmp_path`.
   - `vibecomfy session list` prints the active session id + URL.
   - `vibecomfy session flush default` calls `ComfyClient.free(True, True)` (stub the client).
   - `vibecomfy session stop default` removes the pidfile and url file (the mock SIGTERM handler is replaced with a direct cleanup function).
   - `find_active_session("default")` returns the URL when pidfile is fresh; returns `None` and removes the directory when the recorded pid is dead (use a known-dead pid like `2**31 - 1`).
3. **Add** `tests/test_runtime_run.py` regression: `_cmd_run` with `--runtime auto` and a fake active session in `out/sessions/default/url` calls `run_sync` with that URL **and** constructs the schema provider with the same URL. Stub both `find_active_session` and the underlying `run_sync`/`get_schema_provider` to record arguments.

## Phase 3: Documentation + smoke

### Step 8: Docs (`docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`, `docs/runtime_surface.md`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Append** "Warm sessions" section to `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`: explain `EmbeddedSession` vs `ServerSession`, the `vibecomfy session` CLI, the `auto`/`always`/`never` warm-policy rules, the `/api/free` queue-flag semantics (asynchronous), the `VIBECOMFY_WARM` env var, and the new default routing in `vibecomfy run`.
2. **Append** a `VibeSession` API block to `docs/runtime_surface.md` under "Embedded Surface" (start/run/flush/reconfigure/stop + `SessionConfig` fields).
3. **Update** `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md:3` to reflect that `vibecomfy run` now defaults to `auto` (warm session if available, else embedded one-shot), not `embedded`.

### Step 9: Smoke (`scripts/warm_session_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** a manual smoke script using the **embedded** session (which waits for completion via `queue_prompt_api`) — runs two cheap workflows back-to-back through one `EmbeddedSession`, prints elapsed deltas. Avoids the `/prompt`-returns-after-queue-submission pitfall (FLAG-003): server-mode timing would only measure queue latency, not generation, so this script does not use server mode.
2. **Document** in the docs section that managed-mode warmth is verified at the test layer (spawn count == 1) and that operator-side timing should use embedded mode or external completion polling, not `time vibecomfy run --runtime server`.

## Execution Order

1. Steps 1–4: embedded session + tests first (touches existing public surface; lowest blast radius).
2. Steps 5–7: ServerSession + daemon CLI + warm-default CLI routing + caller-ordering fix for schema provider.
3. Step 8: docs.
4. Step 9: smoke script.
Each phase is independently shippable (no hard-halt prereqs).

## Validation Order

1. `pytest tests/test_runtime_run.py` — confirm the `run_embedded` refactor preserves existing behavior.
2. `pytest tests/test_runtime_session.py` — embedded + server session unit tests with stubs (includes the `wan_t2v.api.json` fingerprint snapshot test).
3. `pytest tests/test_session_cli.py` — daemon CLI + `find_active_session` flow.
4. Full `pytest` — confirm no collateral damage to `test_cli.py`, `test_runpod_matrix.py`, `test_workflow_core.py`, etc.
5. Manual on RunPod (info-only): `python scripts/warm_session_smoke.py` (embedded path) — second `.run()` is materially faster than the first; assert `comfy.model_management.current_loaded_models` is non-empty between the two calls.

      "criterion": "`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` exports `VibeSession` Protocol, `SessionConfig` dataclass, `EmbeddedSession`, `ServerSession`, `find_active_session`, and `model_fingerprint`",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Two sequential `EmbeddedSession.run()` calls share a single `Comfy.__aenter__`/`__aexit__` pair (verified via stubbed FakeComfy enter/exit counts)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`EmbeddedSession.flush()` invokes `Comfy.clear_cache()` exactly once per call",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`EmbeddedSession.reconfigure(SessionConfig(...))` translates SessionConfig fields into the Configuration object passed to `Comfy.reconfigure(...)` (vram_policy, reserve_vram_gb, cache_policy, disable_smart_memory)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`model_fingerprint` extracts loader values stored under positional `widget_*` slots \u2014 proven by a snapshot test that loads `tests/snapshots/wan_t2v.api.json`, mutates `widget_0` of the UNETLoader, and asserts the fingerprint changes",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`model_fingerprint` extracts loader values stored under canonical input names (e.g. `unet_name`, `ckpt_name`) when present",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Auto-flush truth table: same fingerprint + low VRAM \u2192 no flush; different fingerprint + low VRAM \u2192 flush; different fingerprint + high VRAM \u2192 no flush; same loader + different KSampler seed \u2192 no flush",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`VIBECOMFY_WARM=never` flushes before every run regardless of headroom; `VIBECOMFY_WARM=always` never auto-flushes",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`ServerSession.start()` spawns `comfyui serve` once and `ServerSession.run()` reuses the same subprocess across multiple calls (assert spawn count == 1 for N runs \u2014 addresses warm-managed verification)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`ServerSession.start()` translates SessionConfig into the expected CLI argv: `--highvram|--lowvram|--normalvram`, `--reserve-vram <GB>`, `--disable-smart-memory`, `--cache-classic|--cache-lru N|--cache-none`, `--port <P>`",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`ServerSession.flush()` POSTs `/api/free` with payload `{\"unload_models\": true, \"free_memory\": true}`",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`ServerSession.reconfigure()` is a no-op when CLI args are unchanged and stops+starts the subprocess when they differ",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`vibecomfy run` default runtime is `auto`; with an active warm session present, `_cmd_run` calls both `get_schema_provider(\"auto\", server_url=<session_url>)` AND `run_sync(workflow, server_url=<session_url>, ...)` with the same URL",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "When no warm session is active, `vibecomfy run --runtime auto` falls back to embedded one-shot (backwards compatibility)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`vibecomfy session start/stop/list/flush/status` are registered subcommands and exit 0 on the happy path with daemon spawn mocked",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`vibecomfy session start` writes `out/sessions/<id>/pid` and `out/sessions/<id>/url`; `vibecomfy session stop <id>` removes them",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`find_active_session` returns the URL when the recorded pid is alive and returns None (and cleans up) when the pid is dead",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Daemon entrypoint lives in `vibecomfy/commands/session.py` (single file) and is invokable via `python -m vibecomfy.commands.session --daemon --id <NAME> --config <JSON>`",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Existing `tests/test_runtime_run.py` passes unchanged after the `run_embedded` refactor (backwards compatibility)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]

    {

      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`docs/runtime_lifecycle.md` gains a 'Warm sessions' section covering both backends, the CLI, the warm-policy rules, /api/free queue-flag semantics, `VIBECOMFY_WARM`, and the new auto-default routing",
      "priority": "should",

        "read_files",
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`docs/runtime_surface.md` documents the `VibeSession` API (start/run/flush/reconfigure/stop + SessionConfig fields)",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [

  "assumptions": [
    "Sandbox blocks reads outside the project root, so I'm trusting the brief's 'verified facts' about the embedded fork (line numbers, /free payload, CLI flags, MODEL_MANAGEMENT_ARGS reconfigure fingerprint) without re-verifying.",
    "Tests continue to use the existing `sys.modules` injection pattern from `tests/test_runtime_run.py:140-149` to stub `comfy.client.embedded_comfy_client`. No new test deps; pyproject.toml stays at httpx + pytest + pytest-asyncio.",
    "`vibecomfy run --runtime auto` is the new default. Auto-attaches to a warm session if one exists; falls back to embedded one-shot when no session is active (preserves backwards compatibility for users who never run `session start`).",
    "Managed warmth is verified at the test layer via subprocess-spawn-count assertions and `/api/free` payload assertions; v1 does NOT add `/history` or websocket completion polling. The manual operator-side smoke uses the embedded backend (which waits for outputs).",
    "Daemon mode uses `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, '-m', 'vibecomfy.commands.session', '--daemon', ...], start_new_session=True)`. The `--daemon` argparse flag is handled in the same `vibecomfy/commands/session.py` module \u2014 no separate `_session_daemon.py`.",
    "Pidfile + URL file land under `out/sessions/<id>/`. Default session id = 'default'. Stale pidfile auto-cleanup when the recorded pid is dead.",
    "Hard-coded loader-class set for the model fingerprint (9 canonical classes covering ckpt/lora/unet/vae/clip/clip-vision/controlnet/upscale). Each entry lists both canonical input names AND the positional widget_* slots used by VibeWorkflow.compile (vibecomfy/workflow.py:178). Dynamic NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS introspection deferred.",
    "Auto-flush threshold default = 2.0 GB free VRAM. Configurable via `SessionConfig.auto_flush_vram_threshold_gb`.",
    "Fixed port 8188 default for ServerSession (matches existing `runtime/server.py:34`), with `--port` override on `session start`.",
    "`run_embedded` and `run`/`run_sync` retain identical public signatures; existing tests in `tests/test_runtime_run.py` pass unchanged.",
    "Schema provider already accepts `server_url=` (vibecomfy/schema/provider.py:161-178); the only change is moving the `get_schema_provider` call AFTER `find_active_session` in `_cmd_run`."
  ],
  "delta_from_previous_percent": 66.6,
  "structure_warnings": []
}

The repo currently defaults `vibecomfy run` to embedded mode in `vibecomfy/commands/run.py:48`, while the plan only auto-attaches to a warm session for `--runtime server` with no `--server-url`; it does not change the default runtime or otherwise make managed warm sessions the default CLI path.",

    "id": "correctness-1",
    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Model fingerprinting: Checked the proposed hard-coded fingerprint fields against existing compiled workflows and ready-template blocks. `VibeWorkflow.compile()` merges `node.widgets` into API inputs as `widget_*` keys (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:176-179`), and the built-in loader block creates `UNETLoader`, `CLIPLoader`, and `VAELoader` values under `widget_0` (`vibecomfy/blocks/loaders.py:27-47`); current snapshots such as `tests/snapshots/wan_t2v.api.json:30-49` also store those loader filenames under `widget_0`.     "severity": "significant"

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
