  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "subsystem": "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },
  {
    "subsystem": "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-142",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",

# Problem
Today both vibecomfy runtime paths are cold-per-call:
- Embedded: vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:125-138 enters/exits a fresh Comfy() context per run. The exit calls embedded_comfy_client.py:_cleanup -> model_management.unload_all_models() + gc + soft_empty_cache. Models are dumped after every generation.
- Managed server: vibecomfy/runtime/server.py:16 starts comfyui as a subprocess, queues once, terminates. Same outcome at the process level.

ComfyUI itself already keeps models warm by default — current_loaded_models persists in process, load_models_gpu reuses resident copies, eviction is pressure-driven, OOM is auto-handled (execution.py:735-737), and DISABLE_SMART_MEMORY is the only thing that forces unload after each prompt. So the work is structural: stop tearing the runtime down.

# Goal
Add session-aware warm runtime to vibecomfy with two backends sharing one abstraction:
1. Long-lived managed Comfy server (HTTP control plane) — default for CLI users.
2. Long-lived embedded Comfy() context — for in-process callers (runpod worker, tests, tight matrix loops).

Default policy: keep warm. Explicit flush available. Auto-flush on detected model-set change under VRAM pressure.

# Verified facts (don't re-research)
- POST /free and POST /api/free both exist in the embedded hiddenswitch fork at comfy/cmd/server.py:1041 with /api mirror auto-added at :1357. Payload: {"unload_models": bool, "free_memory": bool}. They set queue flags via prompt_queue.set_flag(...) — async, take effect at next prompt boundary, not synchronously.
- CLI flags exist in comfy/cli_args.py:115-160: --highvram, --lowvram, --normalvram, --reserve-vram <GB>, --disable-smart-memory, --cache-classic, --cache-lru N, --cache-none.
- Comfy.clear_cache() (embedded_comfy_client.py:385) and Comfy.reconfigure() (:398) already exposed; reconfigure uses MODEL_MANAGEMENT_ARGS fingerprint to avoid unnecessary subprocess cycling.
- The cleanup that nukes warm state lives in embedded_comfy_client.py:_cleanup (lines ~149-169) and is called from Comfy.__aexit__ (line ~481).

# Design constraints / refinements over the user's notes
- Do NOT default to --highvram (breaks multi-model workflows that need offload). Default to Comfy's smart memory.
- /free is asynchronous — flush API must either drain via a no-op prompt or document the queue-flag semantics. Tests must account for this.
- Auto-flush should only trigger on model-set fingerprint change AND VRAM-headroom-below-threshold. Input/seed/prompt changes must NOT trigger flush.
- VIBECOMFY_WARM=auto|always|never controls policy (auto default).
- Session lifecycle: daemon-style for CLI (pidfile + URL file under out/sessions/<id>/) so multiple `vibecomfy run` invocations share one warm pool. Embedded session is just a Python context manager.
- Browser-only tasks: there are none in this plan (no UI work).
- No hard-halt prereq clauses: each milestone is independently shippable.

# Out of scope for this megaplan
- Multi-GPU session pooling.
- Distributed/remote session sharing across machines.
- Workflow-graph-level caching (Comfy already has --cache-lru / --cache-classic).
- Replacing the existing one-shot run/run_embedded APIs — they should remain as thin wrappers around the new session.
- Cloud/RunPod orchestration changes (the runpod worker can adopt EmbeddedSession in a separate plan).

# Suggested milestone shape (megaplan can refine during prep/plan)
M1: Session abstraction + embedded backend
  - vibecomfy/runtime/session.py: VibeSession protocol + EmbeddedSession holding a long-lived Comfy() across multiple .run(workflow) calls.
  - .flush() -> Comfy.clear_cache(); .reconfigure(config) -> Comfy.reconfigure().
  - Refactor run_embedded() to be a one-shot wrapper around EmbeddedSession.
  - Tests: two sequential runs in one session reuse models (assert via inspecting comfy.model_management.current_loaded_models after each run, or via a stub model manager); flush() drops them; reconfigure with same fingerprint is a no-op.

M2: Managed server backend
  - ServerSession: keeps comfy_server subprocess alive across .run() calls. Start = spawn + wait-ready. .flush() = POST /api/free with appropriate flags. .stop() = SIGTERM with timeout fallback to SIGKILL.
  - Daemon mode: `vibecomfy session start [--id NAME] [--vram-policy ...]` writes pidfile + URL to out/sessions/<id>/; `vibecomfy session stop <id>`; `vibecomfy session list`; `vibecomfy session flush <id>`.
  - `vibecomfy run` auto-discovers active session if one exists, else falls back to one-shot managed mode.
  - Tests: session start/stop, flush via HTTP, two runs share warmth, daemon survives across CLI invocations.

M3: Auto-flush policy + fingerprinting
  - vibecomfy/runtime/model_fingerprint.py: extract loader-node identity tuples from a workflow (CheckpointLoaderSimple.ckpt_name, LoraLoader.lora_name, UNETLoader.unet_name, VAELoader.vae_name, CLIPLoader.clip_name, DualCLIPLoader, etc. — discover the canonical set from the embedded fork's nodes).
  - Session.run() compares current workflow fingerprint to last-run fingerprint. If different AND free VRAM < threshold (configurable, default e.g. 2GB), call flush() before queueing.
  - VIBECOMFY_WARM env var: auto (default) | always | never.
  - Tests: same model + different seed -> no flush; different ckpt -> flush; auto-flush gated by VRAM headroom (mock get_free_memory).

M4: VRAM/cache flag plumbing + docs
  - Session config accepts: vram_policy ("auto"|"high"|"low"|"normal"), reserve_vram_gb, cache_policy ("smart"|"classic"|"lru:N"|"none"), disable_smart_memory.
  - Threaded through to embedded Configuration AND server CLI args.
  - docs/runtime_lifecycle.md: add "Warm sessions" section. docs/runtime_surface.md: document VibeSession API.
  - Smoke test: `vibecomfy session start && vibecomfy run x.json && vibecomfy run y.json && vibecomfy session stop` — second run is materially faster than first (timing-based, with tolerance).

# Open questions for the planning step
- Should `vibecomfy run` lazily start a session on first call and reuse it (implicit warm) vs always require `session start`? Lean toward explicit start for predictability.
- Does the runpod worker (reigh-workspace/reigh-worker) want to consume EmbeddedSession directly or talk to a local managed session? Probably embedded — it owns the process lifecycle already. Out of scope for this plan, but the API should not preclude it.
- Do we need partial flush (just CLIP, just VAE, just UNet)? Probably no — Comfy's pressure-driven eviction handles per-model decisions; only expose all-or-nothing flush at the session boundary.

# Success criteria (suggested — planning phase should refine)
- A `VibeSession` abstraction exists with both Embedded and Server backends.
- Two sequential `.run()` calls in one session share warm models (verified by test, not just timing).
- `flush()` is explicit and works for both backends. For the server backend, the test accounts for /free's queue-flag semantics.
- `vibecomfy session start/stop/list/flush` CLI commands exist and survive across separate `vibecomfy` invocations.
- Auto-flush policy fires on model-set change under VRAM pressure, never on seed/input changes.
- Existing `run` and `run_embedded` one-shot APIs continue to work unchanged (backwards compatibility).
- Documentation updated in docs/runtime_lifecycle.md and docs/runtime_surface.md.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Warm-runtime VibeSession (embedded + managed backends)

## Overview

Both runtime entry points are cold-per-call today: `run_embedded()` enters/exits a fresh `Comfy()` per generation (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:125-138`) whose `__aexit__` runs `unload_all_models()`, and `run()` spawns/terminates `comfyui serve` per call (`vibecomfy/runtime/server.py:27-50`). ComfyUI itself keeps models warm in-process and evicts under pressure — the work here is structural.

We add a `VibeSession` abstraction with two backends — `EmbeddedSession` (one long-lived `Comfy()`) and `ServerSession` (one long-lived `comfyui serve` subprocess) — plus a CLI session pool under `out/sessions/<id>/` (pidfile + URL file) so multiple `vibecomfy run` invocations share one warm pool. Existing `run`/`run_embedded` stay as one-shot wrappers (backwards-compatible).

Per the brief, the long-lived **managed** server is the default for CLI users. The plan changes `vibecomfy run`'s default runtime from `embedded` to `auto` and routes through a discovered session when one exists. The schema provider is taught to share the same URL so we don't accidentally spin up a second cold subprocess for `/object_info`. Default policy: keep-warm with smart memory; explicit `flush()` and a fingerprint-gated auto-flush handle model swaps under VRAM pressure.

Key constraints kept from the brief:
- `/free` is async (queue flag) — `ServerSession.flush()` documents this; tests assert payload + endpoint, not synchronous unload.
- Don't default to `--highvram`.
- Auto-flush gated on **fingerprint change AND** free-VRAM-below-threshold; seed/prompt/input changes never trigger flush.
- Embedded fork not installed locally — tests use the existing `sys.modules` stub pattern from `tests/test_runtime_run.py:140-149`.

Settled decisions carried forward from previous iterations:
- `vibecomfy run --runtime auto` is the new default; auto-attaches to a warm session when one exists, else falls back to embedded one-shot.
- Schema provider URL discovery happens before `get_schema_provider` construction; same URL threaded into both call sites in `_cmd_run`.
- Daemon is folded into `vibecomfy/commands/session.py` (single file), invoked as `python -m vibecomfy.commands.session --daemon --id <NAME> --config <JSON>`.
- Managed-mode warmth verified at the test layer (subprocess spawn count == 1 + `/api/free` payload assertions); operator smoke uses embedded backend.
- ServerSession default port 8188 with `--port` override; auto-flush threshold default 2.0 GB.

Resolutions added in this revision:
- **FLAG-006 / correctness-1 / scope (fingerprint coverage)**: The repo's own snapshots use loader classes outside any reasonable hard-coded list — `LowVRAMCheckpointLoader`, `LowVRAMAudioVAELoader`, `LTXAVTextEncoderLoader`, `DualCLIPLoaderGGUF`, `UnetLoaderGGUF` (verified in `tests/snapshots/ltx2_3_t2v.api.json:94, 125, 383`). Switch from an allowlist to a **pattern-based** extractor: any `class_type` containing the substring `"Loader"` is treated as a model-loading node, and all of its non-edge string-valued input slots (canonical names AND `widget_*` keys) contribute to the fingerprint. Edge references (lists like `["nodeId", outIdx]`) and non-string values are excluded. Plus a small explicit override list (`OVERRIDES`) to add or remove specific class names if needed (initially empty).
- **FLAG-007 / correctness-2 (helper ownership / circular imports)**: Move `RunResult`, `_prepare_prompt`, `_run_metadata`, `_collect_output_paths`, and `_git_sha` from `runtime/run.py` into `runtime/session.py` (one-direction dependency: `run.py` imports from `session.py`, never the reverse). `runtime/__init__.py` re-exports `RunResult` so external imports keep working.
- **all_locations (test_cli.py registry assertion)**: Step 6 explicitly updates `tests/test_cli.py:25-40`'s ordered `COMMANDS` list to include `"session"` in the slot between `"runtime"` and `"logs"`.

Out of scope: multi-GPU pooling, distributed sessions, workflow-graph caching, replacing one-shot APIs, RunPod orchestration changes.

## Phase 1: Embedded session foundation

### Step 1: Add VibeSession protocol + EmbeddedSession + relocate shared helpers (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`, `vibecomfy/runtime/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`. To avoid circular imports between `session.py` and `run.py`, this module **owns** the shared runtime helpers: move `RunResult` (`runtime/run.py:19-25`), `_prepare_prompt` (`runtime/run.py:28-42`), `_run_metadata` (`runtime/run.py:76-96`), `_collect_output_paths` (`runtime/run.py:204-215`), and `_git_sha` (`runtime/run.py:99-109`) from `run.py` into `session.py`. `run.py` imports them from `session.py`; `session.py` does **not** import from `run.py`.
2. **Update** `vibecomfy/runtime/__init__.py:1-11` so `RunResult` is re-exported from `runtime.session` (or the existing `from .run import RunResult` continues to work because `run.py` re-exports). External callers that do `from vibecomfy.runtime import RunResult` keep working.
3. **Add** `SessionConfig` dataclass: `vram_policy: Literal["auto","high","low","normal"] = "auto"`, `reserve_vram_gb: float | None = None`, `cache_policy: Literal["smart","classic","none"] | str = "smart"` (also accepts `"lru:N"`), `disable_smart_memory: bool = False`, `warm_policy: Literal["auto","always","never"] = "auto"`, `auto_flush_vram_threshold_gb: float = 2.0`, `port: int = 8188` (server-only). Class method `SessionConfig.from_workflow_metadata(workflow) -> SessionConfig` reads `workflow.metadata.get("comfy_configuration")` if any (preserves the existing override path).
4. **Add** `class VibeSession(Protocol)`: `async start()`, `async run(workflow, *, backend="api") -> RunResult`, `async flush(*, unload_models=True, free_memory=True)`, `async reconfigure(config)`, `async stop()`, attribute `last_fingerprint`.
5. **Add** `class EmbeddedSession(VibeSession)`: holds one long-lived `Comfy()` across calls. `start()` instantiates and `__aenter__`s once. `run()` calls `comfy.queue_prompt_api(api_dict)` (already waits for completion per `docs/runtime_surface.md:42-46`). `flush()` → `comfy.clear_cache()`. `reconfigure(SessionConfig)` → `comfy.reconfigure(...)` (uses `MODEL_MANAGEMENT_ARGS` fingerprint, so identical configs are no-ops). `stop()` calls `__aexit__(None, None, None)` and tolerates the `model_mmap_residency` AttributeError currently swallowed at `run.py:135-138`.
6. **Move** `_embedded_configuration` from `runtime/run.py:163-181` into `session.py` and extend it to apply `SessionConfig` (vram_policy → highvram/lowvram/normalvram booleans, `reserve_vram_gb` → `reserve_vram`, `cache_policy` → `cache_classic`/`cache_lru`/`cache_none`, `disable_smart_memory`). `VIBECOMFY_COMFY_CONFIGURATION` env override applies last.
7. **Add pattern-based** `model_fingerprint(api_dict) -> tuple` in `session.py`. Algorithm:
   - For each `(node_id, node)` in `api_dict.items()`:
     - Determine if the node is a loader: `is_loader = ("Loader" in node["class_type"]) or (node["class_type"] in OVERRIDES_INCLUDE)` and not in `OVERRIDES_EXCLUDE`.
     - If `is_loader`: for each `(slot_name, value)` in `node["inputs"].items()`:
       - Skip edge references (`isinstance(value, list)` and length 2 with first element `str`/`int`).
       - Skip non-strings (filters out `widget_0` integers in nodes that happen to contain "Loader" in name — none currently, but a defensive filter).
       - Skip empty strings.
       - Emit `(class_type, slot_name, value)` triple.
   - Return `tuple(sorted(triples))`.
   - `OVERRIDES_INCLUDE: frozenset[str] = frozenset()` (empty initially; reserved for non-Loader-suffixed model nodes if discovered later).
   - `OVERRIDES_EXCLUDE: frozenset[str] = frozenset()` (empty initially; reserved if a class contains "Loader" but takes no model file paths).
   - Verified against the repo: covers `CheckpointLoaderSimple`, `LoraLoader`, `UNETLoader`, `VAELoader`, `CLIPLoader`, `DualCLIPLoader`, `CLIPVisionLoader`, `ControlNetLoader`, `UpscaleModelLoader`, **and** `LowVRAMCheckpointLoader`, `LowVRAMAudioVAELoader`, `LTXAVTextEncoderLoader`, `DualCLIPLoaderGGUF`, `UnetLoaderGGUF` (`tests/snapshots/ltx2_3_t2v.api.json:94, 125, 383`).

### Step 2: Auto-flush gate inside `EmbeddedSession.run` (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Compute** `fp = model_fingerprint(api_dict)` before queueing. Resolve effective policy: `os.environ.get("VIBECOMFY_WARM")` overrides `config.warm_policy` if set.
   - `never` → `await self.flush()` then queue.
   - `always` → never auto-flush.
   - `auto` → if `self.last_fingerprint is not None and fp != self.last_fingerprint and _free_vram_gb() < config.auto_flush_vram_threshold_gb`, `await self.flush()` first.
2. After successful queue: `self.last_fingerprint = fp`.
3. **Add** `_free_vram_gb()` lazy-importing `comfy.model_management.get_free_memory()`, converting bytes → GB. Returns `float("inf")` on import/AttributeError.

### Step 3: Refactor `run_embedded` into a one-shot wrapper (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Rewrite** `run_embedded()` (`vibecomfy/runtime/run.py:116-156`) as:
   ```python
   from .session import EmbeddedSession, SessionConfig
   ...
   session = EmbeddedSession(SessionConfig.from_workflow_metadata(workflow))
   await session.start()
   try:
       return await session.run(workflow, backend=backend)
   finally:
       await session.stop()
   ```
   `run_embedded_sync` and the public signature stay unchanged. The `model_mmap_residency` swallowing now lives inside `EmbeddedSession.stop()`.
2. **Delete** the now-relocated helper definitions from `run.py` (`RunResult`, `_prepare_prompt`, `_run_metadata`, `_collect_output_paths`, `_git_sha`, `_embedded_configuration`) and import them from `session.py`. The `run()` and `smoke_runtime()` functions in `run.py` remain (they still own the one-shot managed-server flow).

### Step 4: Tests for the embedded session (`tests/test_runtime_session.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tests/test_runtime_session.py` reusing the `sys.modules` injection pattern from `tests/test_runtime_run.py:140-149`. `FakeComfy` records enter/exit counts plus `queue_prompt_api`/`clear_cache`/`reconfigure` calls.
2. **Cover (must)**:
   - Two `session.run(workflow_a)` calls produce **one** `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` pair.
   - `flush()` calls `clear_cache()` exactly once.
   - `reconfigure(SessionConfig(...))` passes a Configuration whose fields reflect SessionConfig.
   - **Pattern-based fingerprint correctness** — three snapshot tests:
     - Load `tests/snapshots/wan_t2v.api.json`. Same dict twice → same fingerprint. Mutate `widget_0` of `UNETLoader` → different fingerprint. (covers canonical loaders + widget_*)
     - Load `tests/snapshots/ltx2_3_t2v.api.json`. Assert the fingerprint contains tuples for `LowVRAMCheckpointLoader` (line 94), `LowVRAMAudioVAELoader` (line 125), `LTXAVTextEncoderLoader` (line 383), `LoraLoaderModelOnly` (line 359). Mutate `LowVRAMCheckpointLoader.ckpt_name` → fingerprint changes; mutate an unrelated KSampler `widget_0` integer (`tests/snapshots/wan_t2v.api.json:21` style) → fingerprint unchanged.
     - Synthetic dict with a hand-rolled `class_type="DualCLIPLoaderGGUF"` and `class_type="UnetLoaderGGUF"` (matches `ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame.py:172-186`) — assert the values are picked up. Edge references (`[node_id, idx]` lists) on the same loader are NOT included in the fingerprint.
   - Auto-flush truth table with mocked `_free_vram_gb`: same fingerprint + low VRAM → no flush; different fingerprint + low VRAM (0.5 GB) → flush; different fingerprint + high VRAM (10 GB) → no flush; same loaders + different KSampler seed → no flush.
   - `VIBECOMFY_WARM=never` → flush before every run regardless of headroom.
   - `VIBECOMFY_WARM=always` → never auto-flushes regardless of headroom or fingerprint change.
3. **Confirm** existing `tests/test_runtime_run.py` still passes after the `run_embedded` refactor (helpers now live in `session.py` but `run.py` re-imports them; the existing tests use `runtime_run_module._embedded_configuration` and similar which must still resolve via `from .session import _embedded_configuration as _embedded_configuration` in `run.py`).

## Phase 2: Managed server session + warm-default CLI

### Step 5: ServerSession backend (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`, `vibecomfy/runtime/client.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Extract** the subprocess startup from `runtime/server.py:27-41` into a private helper `_spawn_comfy_server(config) -> tuple[Process, str, log_handle]` that translates `SessionConfig` to CLI flags: `--highvram|--lowvram|--normalvram` (skip when `auto`), `--reserve-vram <GB>`, `--disable-smart-memory`, `--cache-classic|--cache-lru N|--cache-none` (skip when `smart`), `--port <P>`. Reuse the existing 120-iteration `/system_stats` readiness loop. Helper lives in `session.py`.
2. **Add** `ServerSession(VibeSession)` to `session.py`. `start()` calls `_spawn_comfy_server`, records `self.url`, `self.process`, `self.log_path`, `self._cli_args`. `run()` calls `ComfyClient(self.url).queue_prompt(api_dict)` and applies the same auto-flush gate as Step 2. `flush()` POSTs `/api/free` with `{"unload_models": True, "free_memory": True}`. `reconfigure(config)` is restart-on-change: rebuild `_cli_args`; if identical, no-op; else `await self.stop(); await self.start()`. `stop()` reuses the SIGTERM/15s/SIGKILL pattern from `server.py:44-50`.
3. **Add** `ComfyClient.free(unload_models: bool, free_memory: bool)` in `runtime/client.py:30+`. One-line docstring noting the queue-async semantics.

### Step 6: Daemon CLI + warm-default routing + registry test update (`vibecomfy/commands/session.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/run.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `vibecomfy/commands/session.py` containing the argparse wiring for `start/stop/list/flush/status` AND the in-process daemon entrypoint. Single file. Module supports being run as `python -m vibecomfy.commands.session --daemon --id NAME --config <json>` (a hidden flag handled before normal argparse routing); when invoked that way it instantiates `ServerSession(SessionConfig.from_dict(...))`, calls `start()`, writes `out/sessions/<id>/{pid,url,config.json}`, installs a SIGTERM handler that calls `await session.stop()` and removes the pidfile, then sleeps on an `asyncio.Event`.
2. **Daemonize** from `session start` by `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-m", "vibecomfy.commands.session", "--daemon", "--id", id_, "--config", json.dumps(config)], stdout=DEVNULL, stderr=open(log_path,"ab"), start_new_session=True)`. Parent waits up to 120 s for `out/sessions/<id>/url` to appear, then exits 0; on timeout, kills the daemon and exits 1.
3. **Subcommands**: `start [--id NAME=default] [--vram-policy auto|high|low|normal] [--reserve-vram-gb F] [--cache-policy smart|classic|lru:N|none] [--disable-smart-memory] [--warm-policy auto|always|never] [--port P=8188]`, `stop <id>`, `list`, `flush <id> [--unload-models/--no-unload-models] [--free-memory/--no-free-memory]`, `status <id>`.
4. **Add** `runtime/session.py::find_active_session(id="default") -> str | None`: read `out/sessions/<id>/url`; if pid file present and `os.kill(pid, 0)` succeeds, return URL; else clean up and return `None`.
5. **Register** in `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py:14-28` by adding `CommandSpec("session", "vibecomfy.commands.session")` between `"runtime"` and `"logs"`.
6. **Update** `tests/test_cli.py:25-40` to add `"session"` to the asserted ordered list (between `"runtime"` and `"logs"` to match the new registry order). This is required to keep the existing `test_cli_command_registry_is_explicit_and_ordered` test passing.
7. **Make warm-managed the default for `vibecomfy run`** (`vibecomfy/commands/run.py`):
   - Change `--runtime` default from `"embedded"` to `"auto"`.
   - At the top of `_cmd_run`, **before** constructing the schema provider: discover the session URL via `find_active_session("default")` (only when `args.runtime in ("auto","server")` and `--server-url` not set). Resolve the effective runtime: `auto` + warm session → server against that URL; `auto` + no warm session → embedded one-shot; `server` + warm session → reuse it; `server` + no warm session → existing one-shot managed.
   - Pass the discovered/explicit URL into `get_schema_provider("auto", server_url=session_url)` so `RuntimeSchemaProvider.object_info_async()` reuses the warm session.
   - Pass the same URL into `run_sync(workflow, server_url=session_url, backend=...)` for the queue submission.

### Step 7: Tests for ServerSession + CLI (`tests/test_runtime_session.py`, `tests/test_session_cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **In** `tests/test_runtime_session.py` add `ServerSession` tests with monkeypatched `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` and a stub `httpx.AsyncClient`. Cover (must):
   - `start()` invokes the subprocess with the expected CLI argv given `SessionConfig(vram_policy="high", reserve_vram_gb=2.0, cache_policy="lru:3", disable_smart_memory=True, port=8200)`.
   - Two `session.run(...)` calls share **one** subprocess (assert spawn count == 1 across N runs).
   - `flush()` POSTs `/api/free` with `{"unload_models": True, "free_memory": True}`.
   - `reconfigure(same_config)` is a no-op. `reconfigure(different_cli_args)` triggers stop+start.
   - `stop()` SIGTERMs once and falls back to SIGKILL when `wait()` exceeds the 15 s timeout.
2. **Create** `tests/test_session_cli.py`. Mock `subprocess.Popen` so the "daemon" is replaced by a synchronous helper that just writes `out/sessions/<id>/{pid,url,config.json}` with a synthetic alive pid. Assert (must):
   - `vibecomfy session start` returns 0 and creates pidfile + url file under `out/sessions/default/` in `tmp_path`.
   - `vibecomfy session list` prints the active session id + URL.
   - `vibecomfy session flush default` calls `ComfyClient.free(True, True)`.
   - `vibecomfy session stop default` removes the pidfile and url file.
   - `find_active_session("default")` returns the URL when pidfile is fresh; returns `None` and removes the directory when the recorded pid is dead.
3. **Add** `tests/test_runtime_run.py` regression: `_cmd_run` with `--runtime auto` and a fake active session in `out/sessions/default/url` calls `run_sync` with that URL **and** constructs the schema provider with the same URL. Stub both `find_active_session` and the underlying `run_sync`/`get_schema_provider` to record arguments.

## Phase 3: Documentation + smoke

### Step 8: Docs (`docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`, `docs/runtime_surface.md`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Append** "Warm sessions" section to `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`: explain `EmbeddedSession` vs `ServerSession`, the `vibecomfy session` CLI, the `auto`/`always`/`never` warm-policy rules, the `/api/free` queue-flag semantics (asynchronous), the `VIBECOMFY_WARM` env var, the pattern-based loader fingerprint (matches any class containing "Loader"), and the new default routing in `vibecomfy run`.
2. **Append** a `VibeSession` API block to `docs/runtime_surface.md` under "Embedded Surface".
3. **Update** `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md:3` to reflect that `vibecomfy run` now defaults to `auto`.

### Step 9: Smoke (`scripts/warm_session_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** a manual smoke script using the **embedded** session (which waits for completion via `queue_prompt_api`) — runs two cheap workflows back-to-back through one `EmbeddedSession`, prints elapsed deltas. Server-mode timing would only measure queue latency, not generation.
2. **Document** that managed-mode warmth is verified at the test layer (spawn count == 1) and that operator-side timing should use embedded mode.

## Execution Order

1. Steps 1–4: embedded session + helper relocation + tests first (touches existing public surface; lowest blast radius).
2. Steps 5–7: ServerSession + daemon CLI + warm-default CLI routing + `test_cli.py` registry update + caller-ordering fix.
3. Step 8: docs.
4. Step 9: smoke script.
Each phase is independently shippable.

## Validation Order

1. `pytest tests/test_cli.py` — confirm registry assertion still passes after adding `"session"` (Step 6.6).
2. `pytest tests/test_runtime_run.py` — confirm the `run_embedded` refactor + helper relocation preserves existing behavior.
3. `pytest tests/test_runtime_session.py` — embedded + server session unit tests, including pattern-based fingerprint snapshot tests against `wan_t2v.api.json` and `ltx2_3_t2v.api.json`.
4. `pytest tests/test_session_cli.py` — daemon CLI + `find_active_session` flow.
5. Full `pytest` — confirm no collateral damage to `test_runpod_matrix.py`, `test_workflow_core.py`, etc.
6. Manual on RunPod (info-only): `python scripts/warm_session_smoke.py` — second `.run()` materially faster; `comfy.model_management.current_loaded_models` non-empty between calls.

        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },

      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
